{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap9961}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
Vérifier que $y_1(x)=e^x$\   et
$y_2(x)=\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{x^k}{k!}$\  sont deux solutions
de l'équation \begin{displaymath}xy''-(x+n)y'+ny=0.\end{displaymath}
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13724}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    xy_1''-(x+n)y_1'+ny_1   & = & x(e^x)''-(x+n)(e^x)'+n(e^x) \\
                            & = & x(e^x)-(x+n)(e^x)+n(e^x) \\
                            & = & 0
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13726}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    \multicolumn{3}{l}{xy_2''-(x+n)y_2'+ny_2} \\
                            & = & x(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{x^k}{k!})''-(x+n)(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{x^k}{k!})'+n(\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{x^k}{k!}) \\
                            & = & x\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{k(k-1)x^{k-2}}{k!}-(x+n)\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{kx^{k-1}}{k!}+n\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{x^k}{k!} \\
                            & = & x\sum\limits_{k=2}^{n} \frac{x^{k-2}}{(k-2)!}-(x+n)\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n} \frac{x^{k-1}}{(k-1)!}+n\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{x^k}{k!} \\
                            & = & x\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n-2} \frac{x^{k}}{k!}-(x+n)\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n-1} \frac{x^{k}}{k!}+n\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{x^k}{k!} \\
                            & = & x\left\lbrack\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{x^{k}}{k!} - \frac{x^{n-1}}{(n-1)!} - \frac{x^{n}}{n!}\right\rbrack -(x+n)\left\lbrack\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{x^{k}}{k!}-\frac{x^{n}}{n!}\right\rbrack +n\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{x^k}{k!} \\
                            & = & \left\lbrack\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{x^{k}}{k!}\right\rbrack\left\lbrack x-(x+n)-n\right\rbrack  - \frac{x^{n}}{(n-1)!} - \frac{x^{n+1}}{n!} + (x+n)\frac{x^{n}}{n!} \\
                            & = & - \frac{x^{n}}{(n-1)!} - \frac{x^{n+1}}{n!} + \frac{x^{n+1}}{n!} + \frac{x^{n}}{(n-1)!} \\
                            & = & 0
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap9979}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
De même, vérifier que $y_1(x)=\sin x$\  et
$y_2(x)=\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{(-1)^kx^{2k+1}}{(2k+1)!}$
sont deux solutions de l'équation différentielle
\begin{displaymath}[xD-(2n+1)](D^2+1\!{\rm l})y=0\end{displaymath}\  où $D = \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}$.
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13729}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    \multicolumn{3}{l}{[xD-(2n+1)](D^2+1\!{\rm l})y_1} \\
    \quad\quad\quad                 & = & [xD-(2n+1)](D^2+1\!{\rm l})\sin x \\
                                    & = & [xD-(2n+1)](\frac{{\rm d^{2}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{2}}\sin x + \sin x) \\
                                    & = & [xD-(2n+1)](-\sin x + \sin x) \\
                                    & = & 0
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13731}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    \multicolumn{3}{l}{[xD-(2n+1)](D^2+1\!{\rm l})y_2} \\
                            & = & [xD-(2n+1)]\left(D^2y_2+y_2\right) \\
                            & = & [xD-(2n+1)]\left(\frac{{\rm d^{2}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{2}} \sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{(-1)^kx^{2k+1}}{(2k+1)!}    +  \sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{(-1)^kx^{2k+1}}{(2k+1)!} \right) \\
                            & = & [xD-(2n+1)]\left(\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}} \sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{(-1)^k(2k+1)x^{2k}}{(2k+1)!}    +  \sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{(-1)^kx^{2k+1}}{(2k+1)!} \right) \\
                            & = & [xD-(2n+1)]\left(\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}} \sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{(-1)^kx^{2k}}{(2k)!}    +  \sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{(-1)^kx^{2k+1}}{(2k+1)!} \right) \\
                            & = & [xD-(2n+1)]\left( \sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{(-1)^k(2k)x^{2k-1}}{(2k)!}    +  \sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{(-1)^kx^{2k+1}}{(2k+1)!} \right) \\
                            & = & [xD-(2n+1)]\left( \sum\limits_{k=1}^{n} \frac{(-1)^kx^{2k-1}}{(2k-1)!}    +  \sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{(-1)^kx^{2k+1}}{(2k+1)!} \right) \\
                            & = & [xD-(2n+1)]\left( \sum\limits_{j=0}^{n-1} \frac{(-1)^{j+1}x^{2j+1}}{(2j+1)!}    +  \sum\limits_{k=0}^{n} \frac{(-1)^kx^{2k+1}}{(2k+1)!} \right) \\
                            & = & [xD-(2n+1)]\left( \sum\limits_{j=0}^{n-1} \frac{(-1)^{j+1}x^{2j+1}}{(2j+1)!} \right. \\
                            &   & \quad\quad\quad \left.+  \sum\limits_{k=0}^{n-1} \frac{(-1)^kx^{2k+1}}{(2k+1)!} + \frac{(-1)^nx^{2n+1}}{(2n+1)!} \right) \\
                            & = & [xD-(2n+1)]\left( \frac{(-1)^nx^{2n+1}}{(2n+1)!} \right) \\
                            & = & x\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}} \frac{(-1)^nx^{2n+1}}{(2n+1)!} -(2n+1)\frac{(-1)^nx^{2n+1}}{(2n+1)!} \\
                            & = & x \frac{(-1)^n(2n+1)x^{2n}}{(2n+1)!} -(2n+1)\frac{(-1)^nx^{2n+1}}{(2n+1)!} \\
                            & = & 0
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10029}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
Déterminer une fonction $Q(x)$\  pour que les fonctions
$\sin\log x$\  et $\cos\log x$\  avec $x>0$\  soient des solutions de
l'équation \begin{displaymath}D(Q(x)\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y) + \frac{y}{x} = 0\end{displaymath}\  où $D=\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}$.
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13754}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    D(Q(x)\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y) + \frac{y}{x} & = & D(Q(x)y') + \frac{y}{x} \\
                                    & = & D(Q(x)(ay_1+by_2)') + \frac{(ay_1+by_2)}{x} \\
                                    & = & D(Q(x)\frac{ay_2-by_1}{x}) + \frac{-axy_2'+bxy_1')}{x} \\
                                    & = & 0
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10061}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
Déterminer toutes les fonctions $Q(t)$\  telles que la fonction
$y(t)=\frac{t}{1+t^2}$\  soit une solution de l'équation
$\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}(Qy) + 2ty=1$.
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13779}%
$ \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}(Qy) + 2ty=1 \Rightarrow \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}(Qy) = 1 - 2ty = 1-2t\frac{t}{1+t^2}
= \frac{(1+t^2)-2t^2}{1+t^2} = \frac{1-t^2}{1+t^2} = \frac{-(1+t^2)+2}{1+t^2}
= -1 + \frac{2}{1+t^2}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10085}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
En << devinant >> que l'équation $y''-3y'-4y=0$\  admet une
solution de la forme $y=e^{ax}$\  pour une certaine constante
$a$, trouver deux solutions de cette équation.
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13792}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    y''-3y'-4y=0    & \Rightarrow & (e^{ax})''-3(e^{ax})'-4(e^{ax})=0 \\
                    & \Rightarrow & a^2e^{ax}-3ae^{ax}-4e^{ax}=0 \\
                    & \Rightarrow & e^{ax}(a^2-3a-4)=0 \\
                    & \Rightarrow & a^2-3a-4=0 \\
                    & \Rightarrow & (a-4)(a+1)=0 \\
                    & \Rightarrow & a=-1 \textrm{ ou } a=4
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13794}%
$ y=e^{-x}$%

\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13796}%

\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10097}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
Supposons que $Q(x)$\  est une solution de \begin{displaymath}\frac{{\rm d^{2}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{2}}y +
y\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}=x^3\end{displaymath}\  satisfaisant $y(-1)=1$\  et $y'(-1)=2$, trouver
les valeurs de $Q''(-1)$\  et $Q'''(-1)$.
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13799}%
$ Q''(x)+Q(x)Q'(x) = x^3$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13801}%
$ Q''(-1) = (-1)^3  - Q(-1)Q'(-1) = -1 - 1 \cdot 2 = -3$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13803}%
$ Q''(x)= x^3 - Q(x)Q'(x) \Rightarrow Q'''(x) = 3x^2 - Q(x)Q''(x) - (Q'(x))^2$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13805}%
$ Q'''(-1) = 3(-1)^2 - Q(-1)Q''(-1) - (Q'(-1))^2 = 3-(1)(-3) - (2)^2 = 2$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10115}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
Trouver la solution générale des équations suivantes :
\begin{description}
\item[{{i)}}] $\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y=(1+t)(1+y)$
\item[{{ii)}}] $(1+t^2)\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y=1+y^2$
\end{description}
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13816}%
$ y \neq -1$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13818}%
$ \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y=(1+t)(1+y)$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13820}%
$ \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y=\frac{g(t)}{h(y)}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13822}%
$ g(t)=(1+t)$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13824}%
$ h(y)=1/(1+y)$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13826}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y=(1+t)(1+y)   & \Rightarrow & \frac{1}{1+y}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y=(1+t) \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \int \frac{1}{1+y}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y\;{\rm d}t =\int (1+t)\;{\rm d}t \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \int \frac{1}{1+y}\;{\rm d}y =\int (1+t)\;{\rm d}t \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \ln |1+y| = t+\frac{t^2}{2} + c \\
                                & \Rightarrow & |1+y| = e^{t+\frac{t^2}{2} + c} \\
                                & \Rightarrow & |1+y| = e^{t+\frac{t^2}{2}}e^{c} \\
                                & \Rightarrow & |1+y| = ke^{t+\frac{t^2}{2}} \\
                                & \Rightarrow & y = ke^{t+\frac{t^2}{2}} - 1
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13830}%
$ y \equiv -1$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13832}%
$ y = ke^{t+\frac{t^2}{2}} - 1$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13836}%
$ (1+t^2)\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y=1+y^2$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13838}%
$ 1+t^2$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13840}%
$ 1+x^2$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13842}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        (1+t^2)\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y=1+y^2 & \Rightarrow & \frac{1}{1+y^2}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y=\frac{1}{1+t^2} \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \int \frac{1}{1+y^2}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y\;{\rm d}t =\int \frac{1}{1+t^2}\;{\rm d}t \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \arctan y = \arctan t + c
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13844}%
$ t=0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13846}%
$ \arctan y(0) = \arctan 0 +
c= c$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13848}%
$ \arctan y = \arctan t + \arctan y(0)$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13850}%
$ \tan$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13852}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        y & = & \tan (\arctan t + \arctan y(0)) \\
          & = & \frac{\tan\arctan t + \tan\arctan y(0)}{1-\tan\arctan t \cdot \tan\arctan y(0)} \\
          & = & \frac{t+y(0)}{1-ty(0)},
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13854}%
$ t \neq \frac{1}{y(0)}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10187}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
Déterminer un intervalle d'existence pour la solution au
problème suivant : \begin{displaymath}t^2(1+y^2)+2y\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y=0 ; \quad\quad y(0)=1.\end{displaymath}
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13857}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    t^2(1+y^2)+2y\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y=0   & \Rightarrow & 2y\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y = -t^2(1+y^2) \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \frac{2y}{1+y^2}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y = -t^2 \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}} \ln |1+y^2| = -t^2 \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}} \ln 1+y^2 = -t^2 \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \ln 1+y^2 = -\frac{t^3}{3} + c \\
                                & \Rightarrow & 1+y^2 = e^{-\frac{t^3}{3}+c} \\
                                & \Rightarrow & 1+y^2 = e^{-\frac{t^3}{3}}e^{c} \\
                                & \Rightarrow & 1+y^2 = ke^{-\frac{t^3}{3}} \\
                                & \Rightarrow & y = \sqrt{ke^{-\frac{t^3}{3}} - 1}
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13859}%
$ ke^{-\frac{t^3}{3}} - 1>0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13861}%
$ ke^{-\frac{t^3}{3}} - 1=0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13863}%
$ y=0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13865}%
$ y(0)=1$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13869}%
$ 1 = y(0) = \sqrt{ke^{-\frac{0^3}{3}} - 1} = \sqrt{k-1}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13871}%

\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13875}%
$ 2e^{-\frac{t^3}{3}} > 1$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13877}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    2e^{-\frac{t^3}{3}} > 1 & \Rightarrow & e^{-\frac{t^3}{3}} > 1/2 \\
                            & \Rightarrow & -\frac{t^3}{3} > \ln 1/2 \\
                            & \Rightarrow & -\frac{t^3}{3} > -\ln 2 \\
                            & \Rightarrow & \frac{t^3}{3} < \ln 2 \\
                            & \Rightarrow & t < \sqrt[3]{3\ln 2}
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10229}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
On appelle \emph{équation différentielle homogène} du premier
ordre une équation de la forme \begin{displaymath}(1)\quad\quad \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y =
f(\frac{y}{t}).\end{displaymath}
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10235}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
Montrer qu'en posant $u=\frac{y}{t}$\  on obtient une équation
différentielle à variables séparables
\begin{displaymath}\frac{1}{f(u)-u}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}u=\frac{1}{t} \textrm{ si }
f(u)\neq u.\end{displaymath}
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13885}%
$ u=\frac{y}{t} \Rightarrow y=ut \Rightarrow y'=u + tu'$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13887}%
$ \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y = f(\frac{y}{t})$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13889}%
$ u+tu' = y' =
f(\frac{y}{t}) = f(u)$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13891}%
$ u'=\frac{f(u)-u}{t}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13893}%
$ f(u) \neq u$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10249}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
En utilisant la partie a), résoudre l'équation différentielle
\begin{displaymath}(x^3-y^3)\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y=x^2y.\end{displaymath}
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13896}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    (x^3-y^3)\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y=x^2y    & \Rightarrow & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y=\frac{x^2y}{(x^3-y^3)} \\\\
                                & \Rightarrow & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y=\frac{x^3 \cdot y/x}{x^3(1-y^3/x^3)} \\\\
                                & \Rightarrow & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y=\frac{y/x}{1-(y/x)^3}
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13898}%
$ u=\frac{y}{x}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13900}%
$ y=ux \Rightarrow y'=u+xu'$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13902}%
$ u+xu' = y' = \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y = f(\frac{y}{x}) = f(u) =
\frac{u}{1-u^3}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13904}%
$ u'=\frac{\frac{u}{1-u^3}-u}{x}
= \frac{\frac{u}{1-u^3}-\frac{u-u^4}{1-u^3}}{x} =
\frac{\frac{u^4}{1-u^3}}{x}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13906}%
$ u \neq 0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13908}%
$ u\equiv 0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13910}%
$ y=xu=0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13914}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    u' = \frac{\frac{u^4}{1-u^3}}{x}    & \Rightarrow & \frac{1-u^3}{u^4}u' = \frac{1}{x} \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & (\frac{1}{u^4}-\frac{1}{u})u' = \frac{1}{x} \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & -\frac{1}{3u^3}-\ln |u| = \ln |x| + c \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & -\frac{1}{3(y/x)^3}-\ln |y/x| = \ln |x| + c \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & -\frac{1}{3(y/x)^3}-\ln |y/x| - \ln |x| = c \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & -\frac{1}{3(y/x)^3}-\ln |y| = c \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \ln |y| = -\frac{1}{3(y/x)^3} - c \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & |y| = e^{-\frac{1}{3(y/x)^3} - c} \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & |y| = e^{-\frac{1}{3(y/x)^3}}e^{-c} \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & |y| = ke^{-\frac{1}{3(y/x)^3}} \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & y = k'e^{-\frac{x^3}{3y^3}}
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13916}%
$ y$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10287}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}

alors $y=u_0t$\  est une solution de (1). Voyez si vous avez obtenu cette solution dans la partie b).
\end{minipage}}%

\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13927}%
$ y \equiv 0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10297}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
Trouver la famille de courbes orthogonales à la famille
$x^2+y^2=cx$\  avec $c \in \mathbb{R}$. Observer que l'ED à
résoudre est homogène. De plus, illustrer graphiquement votre
résultat.
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13930}%
$ (x,y)$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13932}%
$ x^2+y^2=cx$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13936}%
$ 2x+2yy'=c \Rightarrow
y'=\frac{c-2x}{2y}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13938}%
$ x^2+y^2=cx \Rightarrow c = x +
\frac{y^2}{x}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13940}%
$ x \neq 0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13944}%
$ (0,0)$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13946}%
$ y'=\frac{c-2x}{2y} = \frac{(x + \frac{y^2}{x})-2x}{2y}
= \frac{\frac{y^2}{x}-x}{2y} = \frac{y^2-x^2}{2xy}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13948}%
$ y'$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13950}%
$ y' = \frac{2xy}{x^2-y^2} = \frac{x^2\cdot 2(y/x)}{x^2(1-(y/x)^2)}
= \frac{2(y/x)}{1-(y/x)^2}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13956}%
$ u+xu' = y' = \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y = f(\frac{y}{x}) = f(u) =
\frac{2u}{1-u^2}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13958}%
$ u'=\frac{\frac{2u}{1-u^2}-u}{x}
= \frac{\frac{2u}{1-u^2}-\frac{u-u^3}{1-u^2}}{x} =
\frac{\frac{u+u^3}{1-u^2}}{x}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13960}%
$ u+u^3 \neq 0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13962}%
$ u(1+u^2) \neq 0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13972}%
$ u' = \frac{\frac{u+u^3}{1-u^2}}{x} \Rightarrow \frac{1-u^2}{u+u^3}u' =
\frac{1}{x}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13974}%
$ \frac{1-u^2}{u+u^3} = \frac{1}{u} -
2\frac{u}{1+u^2}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13976}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    u' = \frac{\frac{u+u^3}{1-u^2}}{x}  & \Rightarrow & \frac{1-u^2}{u+u^3}u' = \frac{1}{x} \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & (\frac{1}{u}-2\frac{u}{1+u^2})u' = \frac{1}{x} \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \ln |u| - \ln |1+u^2| = \ln |x| + c \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \ln |\frac{u}{1+u^2}| = \ln |x| + c \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \frac{u}{1+u^2} = kx \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \frac{y/x}{1+(y/x)^2} = kx \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \frac{xy}{x^2+y^2} = kx \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \frac{y}{x^2+y^2} = k \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & x^2+y^2 = k'y \\\\
                                        & \Rightarrow & (x-0)^2+(y-k'')^2 = k'''
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10325}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
Écrivez, à l'aide de l'opérateur $\Delta$, l'équation
$x_{n+4}-5x_{n+2} + 4x_n=-12n-4$.
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13985}%
$ F(x_n)=x_{n+1}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13987}%
$ F^k(x_n)=x_{n+k}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13989}%
$ k \in \mathbb{N}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13991}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    \multicolumn{3}{l}{x_{n+4}-5x_{n+2} + 4x_n=-12n-4} \\
    \quad\quad                      & \Rightarrow & F^4(x_n)-5F^2(x_n) + 4 1\!{\rm l}(x_n)=-12n-4 \\
                                    & \Rightarrow & \left(F^4-5F^2 + 4 1\!{\rm l}\right)(x_n)=-12n-4.
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13993}%
$ F(x_n)=x_{n+1} = (x_{n+1}-x_n)+x_n = \Delta(x_n) + 1\!{\rm l}(x_n)
= (\Delta + 1\!{\rm l})(x_n)$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath13995}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    \multicolumn{3}{l}{x_{n+4}-5x_{n+2} + 4x_n=-12n-4} \\
        \quad\quad  & \Rightarrow & \left(F^4-5F^2 + 4 1\!{\rm l}\right)(x_n)=-12n-4 \\
                    & \Rightarrow & \left((\Delta + 1\!{\rm l})^4-5(\Delta + 1\!{\rm l})^2 + 4 1\!{\rm l}\right)(x_n)=-12n-4 \\
                    & \Rightarrow & \left((\Delta^4+4\Delta^3+6\Delta^2+4\Delta+1\!{\rm l})\right.  \\
                    &             & \quad\quad \left. -5(\Delta^2+2\Delta+1\!{\rm l})^2 + 4 1\!{\rm l}\right)(x_n)=-12n-4 \\
                    & \Rightarrow & \left(\Delta^4+4\Delta^3+\Delta^2-6\Delta\right)(x_n)=-12n-4
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10365}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
Soit \begin{displaymath}I_k=\int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\cos kt - \cos kx}{\cos
t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t;\quad\quad k=0,1,2,\ldots\end{displaymath}
\par
Montrer que $I_k(x)$\  est une solution de l'équation
\begin{displaymath}I_{k+1}(x)-2I_k(x)\cos x + I_{k-1}(x)=0\end{displaymath}\  pour $k \geqslant
1$\  et que $I_0(x)=0$\  et $I_1(x)=\pi$.
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline13998}%
$ I_0(x)=\int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\cos 0t - \cos 0x}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t
= \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{0}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t = 0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14000}%
$ I_1(x)=\int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\cos 1t - \cos 1x}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t
= \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} 1\;{\rm d}t = \pi$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14002}%
$ k\geqslant 1$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14004}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    \multicolumn{3}{l}{I_{k+1}(x)-2I_k(x)\cos x + I_{k-1}(x)} \\
    \quad   & = & \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\cos (k+1)t - \cos (k+1)x}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t \\
            &   & \quad - 2 \cos x \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\cos kt - \cos kx}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t \\
            &   & \quad\quad + \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\cos (k-1)t - \cos (k-1)x}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t \\
            & = & \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\cos (kt+t) - \cos (kx+x)}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t \\
            &   & \quad - 2 \cos x\int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\cos kt - \cos kx}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t \\
            &   & \quad\quad + \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\cos (kt-t) - \cos (kx-x)}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t \\
            & = & \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\left\lbrack\cos kt\cos t - \sin kt \sin t \right\rbrack - \left\lbrack\cos kx\cos x - \sin kx \sin x \right\rbrack}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t \\
            &   & \quad - 2 \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\cos kt\cos x - \cos kx\cos x}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t \\
            &   & \quad\quad + \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\left\lbrack\cos kt\cos(-t) - \sin kt \sin(-t) \right\rbrack - \left\lbrack\cos kx\cos(-x) - \sin kx \sin(-x) \right\rbrack}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t \\
            & = & \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\left\lbrack\cos kt\cos t - \sin kt \sin t \right\rbrack - \left\lbrack\cos kx\cos x - \sin kx \sin x \right\rbrack}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t \\
            &   & \quad - 2 \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\cos kt\cos x - \cos kx\cos x}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t \\
            &   & \quad\quad + \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{\left\lbrack\cos kt\cos t + \sin kt \sin t \right\rbrack - \left\lbrack\cos kx\cos x + \sin kx \sin x  \right\rbrack}{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t \\
            & = & \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{2\cos kt\cos t - 2\cos kt\cos x }{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t \\
            & = & \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} \frac{2\cos kt \left\lbrack \cos t - \cos x\right\rbrack }{\cos t - \cos x}\;{\rm d}t \\
            & = & \int\limits_{0}^{\pi} 2\cos kt\;{\rm d}t \\
            & = & \frac{2}{k}\sin kt\bigg\vert_{0}^{\pi} \\
            & = & 0
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage

\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
Soient $a>0$, $b>0$\  et $c>0$. Suivant la valeur des paramètres
$a,b$\  et $c$, donner les différentes représentations
graphiques possibles pour la solution de l'équation aux
différences finies $ax_n+bx_{n-1}=c$. Noter que six cas sont
possibles. Bien identifier chacun de ces six cas.
\end{minipage}}%

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14007}%
$ x_n=\frac{-b}{a}x_{n-1} + \frac{c}{a}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14009}%
$ a>0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14011}%
$ z_0=x_0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14013}%
$ z_{n+1}=\frac{x_{n+1}}{\left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^n}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14015}%
$ z_{n+1}=\frac{x_{n+1}}{\left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^n} = \frac{\frac{-b}{a}x_{n-1} + \frac{c}{a}}{\left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^n} = \frac{x_n}{\left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}} + \frac{\frac{c}{a}}{\left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^n} = z_n + \frac{\frac{c}{a}}{\left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^n} = z_n + \frac{c}{a}\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)^n$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_indisplay14017}%
$\displaystyle \left\lbrace
\begin{array}{rcl}
z_1-z_0 & = & \frac{c}{a}\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)^0 \\
z_2-z_1 & = & \frac{c}{a}\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)^1 \\
z_3-z_2 & = & \frac{c}{a}\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)^2 \\
& \vdots & \\
z_n-z_{n-1} & = & \frac{c}{a}\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)^{n-1}
\end{array}
\right.$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14019}%
$ z_n-z_0=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}\frac{c}{a}\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)^{i}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14021}%
$ z_n=z_0 + \sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}\frac{c}{a}\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)^{i}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14023}%
$ x_n = \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}z_n = \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}x_0 + \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}\frac{c}{a}\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)^{i}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14025}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    x_n & = & \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}x_0 + \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}\frac{c}{a}\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)^{i} \\
            & = & \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}x_0 + \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}\frac{c}{a}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)^{i} \\
            & = & \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}x_0 + \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}\frac{c}{a} \cdot \frac{1-\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)^{n}}{1-\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)} \\
            & = & \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}x_0 + \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}\frac{c}{a} \cdot \left(1-\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)^{n}\right) \frac{b}{a+b} \\
            & = & \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}x_0 - \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n}\frac{c}{a+b} \cdot \left(1-\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)^{n}\right)  \\
            & = & \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}x_0 - \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n}\frac{c}{a+b} +\frac{c}{a+b}  \\& = & \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n} \left(\frac{-a}{b}x_0 - \frac{c}{a+b}\right) +\frac{c}{a+b}  \\
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14031}%
$ x_0=\frac{-bc}{a(a+b)}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14033}%

\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14041}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        x_n & = & \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}x_0 + \left( \frac{-b}{a} \right)^{n-1}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}\frac{c}{a}\left( \frac{-a}{b} \right)^{i} \\
                & = & (-1)^{n-1}x_0 + (-1)^{n-1}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}\frac{c}{a}(-1)^{i}.
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14043}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        x_0 & = & x_0 \\
        x_1 & = & x_0+\frac{c}{a} \\
        x_2 & = & -x_0 \\
        x_3 & = & x_0+\frac{c}{a} \\
        x_4 & = & -x_0 \\
        & \vdots & \\
        x_{2n-1} & = & x_0+\frac{c}{a} \\
        x_{2n} & = & -x_0
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10471}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
Soit l'ED $\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y +p(x)y=q(x)$\  où $p(x)$\  et $q(x)$\  sont
des fonctions continues de $x$. Supposons que $q(x)$\  est une
solution de l'équation homogène associée à cete équation.
Montrer qu'alors la fonction $y(x)=xq(x)$\  est une solution
particulière de l'équation.
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14088}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}\left(xq(x)\right) +p(x)(xq(x))    & = & \left(q(x) + xq'(x)\right) +xp(x)q(x) \\
                                                & = & q(x) + x \left\lbrack q'(x) + p(x)q(x) \right\rbrack \\
                                                & = & q(x) + x \cdot 0 \\
                                                & = & q(x)
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14090}%
$ q(x)$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlpictureA{tex2html_wrap10487}%
\framebox{\begin{minipage}[t]{12cm}
Donner, en vous inspirant de la partie a), la solution
générale de l'équation
\begin{displaymath}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y-\frac{1}{1-x^2}y=\sqrt{\frac{1+x}{1-x}}\end{displaymath}\  où
$-1<x<1$.
\end{minipage}}%
\lthtmlpictureZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14093}%
$ q(x) = \sqrt{\frac{1+x}{1-x}}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14097}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    q'(x)   & = & \frac{1}{2\sqrt{\frac{1+x}{1-x}}}\left(\frac{1+x}{1-x}\right)' \\\\
            & = & \frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1-x}{1+x}}\frac{(1-x)-(1+x)(-1)}{(1-x)^2} \\\\
            & = & \frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{1-x}{1+x}}\frac{2}{(1-x)^2} \\\\
            & = & \frac{1}{\sqrt{(1+x)(1-x)^3}}
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14103}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y-\frac{1}{1-x^2}y & = & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}q(x)-\frac{1}{1-x^2}q(x) \\
                                & = & \frac{1}{\sqrt{(1+x)(1-x)^3}} - \frac{1}{1-x^2}\sqrt{\frac{1+x}{1-x}} \\
                                & = & 0
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14120}%
$ (1+t^2)\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y+2ty=1 \Rightarrow \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y+\frac{2t}{1+t^2}y=\frac{1}{1+t^2}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14122}%

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_indisplay14124}%
$\displaystyle \mu(t)=e^{\int p(t)\;{\rm d}t} = e^{\int \frac{2t}{1+t^2}\;{\rm d}t} = e^{\ln (1+t^2)}=1+t^2.$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14126}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    (1+t^2)\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y + 2ty = 1 & \Rightarrow & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}\left\lbrack (1+t^2)y \right\rbrack = 1 \\
                                & \Rightarrow & (1+t^2)y = t+c \\
                                & \Rightarrow & y = \frac{t+c}{1+t^2}.
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_indisplay14133}%
$\displaystyle \mu(t)=e^{\int p(t)\;{\rm d}t} = e^{\int 1\;{\rm d}t} = e^{t}.$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14135}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    e^t\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y + e^{t}y = te^{2t}    & \Rightarrow & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}\left\lbrack e^ty \right\rbrack = te^{2t} \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & e^ty = \int te^{2t}\; {\rm d}t \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & e^ty = t\frac{e^{2t}}{2} - \int \frac{e^{2t}}{2}\; {\rm d}t \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & e^ty = t\frac{e^{2t}}{2} - \frac{e^{2t}}{4} + c \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & e^ty = \frac{e^{2t}}{2}\left(t-\frac{1}{2}\right) + c \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & y = \frac{e^{t}}{2}\left(t-\frac{1}{2}\right) + \frac{c}{e^t}.
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

                                                & \Rightarrow & e^{-t^2}y = \int te^{-t^2}\; {\rm d}t \\
                                                & \Rightarrow & e^{-t^2}y = -\frac{1}{2}e^{-t^2} + c  \\
                                                & \Rightarrow & y = -\frac{1}{2} + ce^{t^2}.
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%

\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

$\displaystyle \mu(t)=e^{\int p(t)\;{\rm d}t} = e^{\int \frac{4t}{1+t^2}\;{\rm d}t} = e^{2\ln (1+t^2)}=(1+t^2)^2.$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14161}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    \multicolumn{3}{l}{(1+t^2)^2\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y + 4t(1+t^2)y = t(1+t^2)} \\
    \quad\quad  & \Rightarrow & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}\left\lbrack (1+t^2)^2y \right\rbrack = t(1+t^2) \\
                & \Rightarrow & (1+t^2)^2y = \int t(1+t^2)\; {\rm d}t \\
                & \Rightarrow & (1+t^2)^2y = \frac{t^2}{2} + \frac{t^4}{4} + c \\
                & \Rightarrow & y = \frac{t^2}{2(1+t^2)^2} + \frac{t^4}{4(1+t^2)^2} + \frac{c}{(1+t^2)^2}.
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14169}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    y & = & \frac{t^2}{2(1+t^2)^2} + \frac{t^4}{4(1+t^2)^2} + \frac{1}{4(1+t^2)^2} \\\\
      & = & \frac{2t^2}{4(1+t^2)^2} + \frac{t^4}{4(1+t^2)^2} + \frac{1}{4(1+t^2)^2} \\\\
      & = & \frac{t^4+2t^2+1}{4(1+t^2)^2} \\\\
      & = & \frac{(t^2+1)^2}{4(1+t^2)^2} \\\\
      & = & \frac{1}{4}.
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14178}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y+\sqrt{1+t^2}e^{-t}y=0    & \Rightarrow & \mu(t)\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y+\mu(t)\sqrt{1+t^2}e^{-t}y=0 \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}\left\lbrack \mu(t)y \right\rbrack = 0 \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \mu(t)y = \int 0\; {\rm d}t \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \mu(t)y = c \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & y = \frac{c}{\mu(t)}
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%

    \quad\quad  & \Rightarrow & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}\left\lbrack xy \right\rbrack = \frac{1+2x^2}{x} \\
                & \Rightarrow & xy = \int \frac{1+2x^2}{x}\; {\rm d}x \\
                & \Rightarrow & xy = \ln |x| + x^2 + c \\
                & \Rightarrow & y = \frac{1}{x}\ln |x| + x + \frac{c}{x} \\
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14242}%
$ u' = \frac{\frac{(u+2)(u+1)}{2-u}}{x} \Rightarrow \frac{2-u}{(u+2)(u+1)}u' =
\frac{1}{x}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14244}%
$ \frac{2-u}{(u+2)(u+1)} = \frac{3}{u+1} -
\frac{4}{u+2}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14246}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    u' = \frac{\frac{(u+2)(u+1)}{2-u}}{x}   & \Rightarrow & \frac{2-u}{(u+2)(u+1)}u' = \frac{1}{x} \\\\
                                            & \Rightarrow & (\frac{3}{u+1}-\frac{4}{u+2})u' = \frac{1}{x} \\\\
                                            & \Rightarrow & 3\ln |u+1| - 4\ln |u+2| = \ln |x| + c \\\\
                                            & \Rightarrow & \ln |u+1|^3 - \ln |u+2|^4 - \ln |x| = c \\\\
                                            & \Rightarrow & \ln |\frac{(u+1)^3}{x(u+2)^4}| = c \\\\
                                            & \Rightarrow & \frac{(u+1)^3}{x(u+2)^4} = k \\\\
                                            & \Rightarrow & \frac{(\frac{y}{x}+1)^3}{x(\frac{y}{x}+2)^4} = k \\\\
                                            & \Rightarrow & \frac{(\frac{y+x}{x})^3}{x(\frac{y+2x}{x})^4} = k \\\\
                                            & \Rightarrow & \frac{(y+x)^3}{(y+2x)^4} = k
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

        \quad\quad\quad                             & = & x\ln x \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}\left( A\ln x + Bx + C \right) + \left( A\ln x + Bx + C \right) \\
                                                    & = & x\ln x \left( \frac{A}{x} + B \right) + \left( A\ln x + Bx + C \right) \\
                                                    & = & \left( 2A \right)\ln x + \left( B \right) x + \left(B\right)x\ln x + \left( C \right)
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14267}%
$ x_{n+1} = 3x_n + \frac{3^n}{3} +  \frac{4^n}{3} - \frac{n^2}{3} - \frac{1}{3}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14271}%
$ z_{n+1}=\frac{x_{n+1}}{3^n}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14273}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        z_{n+1} & = & \frac{x_{n+1}}{3^n} \\
                & = & \frac{3x_n + \frac{3^n}{3} +  \frac{4^n}{3} - \frac{n^2}{3} - \frac{1}{3}}{3^n} \\
                & = & \frac{x_n}{3^{n-1}} + 3^{-n}\left(\frac{3^n}{3} +  \frac{4^n}{3} - \frac{n^2}{3} - \frac{1}{3}\right) \\
                & = & z_n + 3^{-n}\left(\frac{3^n}{3} +  \frac{4^n}{3} - \frac{n^2}{3} - \frac{1}{3}\right).
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_indisplay14275}%
$\displaystyle \left\lbrace
\begin{array}{rcl}
z_1-z_0 & = & 3^{-0}\left(\frac{3^0}{3} +  \frac{4^0}{3} - \frac{0^2}{3} - \frac{1}{3}\right) \\
z_2-z_1 & = & 3^{-1}\left(\frac{3^1}{3} +  \frac{4^1}{3} - \frac{1^2}{3} - \frac{1}{3}\right) \\
z_3-z_2 & = & 3^{-2}\left(\frac{3^2}{3} +  \frac{4^2}{3} - \frac{2^2}{3} - \frac{1}{3}\right) \\
& \vdots & \\
z_n-z_{n-1} & = & 3^{-(n-1)}\left(\frac{3^{n-1}}{3} +  \frac{4^{n-1}}{3} - \frac{(n-1)^2}{3} - \frac{1}{3}\right)
\end{array}
\right.$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_indisplay14277}%
$\displaystyle z_n-z_0 = \sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1} 3^{-i}\left(\frac{3^i}{3} +  \frac{4^i}{3} - \frac{i^2}{3} - \frac{1}{3}\right).$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_indisplay14279}%
$\displaystyle z_n=z_0 + \sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1} 3^{-i}\left(\frac{3^i}{3} +  \frac{4^i}{3} - \frac{i^2}{3} - \frac{1}{3}\right).$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_indisplay14281}%
$\displaystyle x_n=3^{n-1}z_n = 3^{n-1}\left\lbrack z_0 + \sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1} 3^{-i}\left(\frac{3^i}{3} +  \frac{4^i}{3} - \frac{i^2}{3} - \frac{1}{3}\right)\right\rbrack$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14284}%
$ x>0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14286}%
$ \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y = \frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{2}\left( \frac{y}{x}\right)^2$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14290}%
$ y=xu
\Rightarrow y'=u+xu'$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14292}%
$ y'=\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y = \frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{2}\left(
\frac{y}{x}\right)^2 = \frac{1}{2} +
\frac{1}{2}u^2$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14294}%
$ u+xu'=\frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{2}u^2
\Rightarrow u'=\frac{\frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{2}u^2 - u}{x} =
\frac{\frac{1}{2}(u-1)^2}{x}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14296}%
$ u \neq 1$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14298}%
$ u=1$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14300}%
$ y=x$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14302}%
$ y(e)=0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14306}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    u'= \frac{\frac{1}{2}(u-1)^2}{x} & \Rightarrow & \frac{2}{(u-1)^2}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}u = \frac{1}{x} \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \int \frac{2}{(u-1)^2}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}u\;{\rm d}x =\int \frac{1}{x}\;{\rm d}x \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \int \frac{2}{(u-1)^2}\;{\rm d}u =\int \frac{1}{x}\;{\rm d}x \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \frac{-2}{u-1} = \ln x + c \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \frac{-2}{\ln x + c} + 1 = u  \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \frac{y}{x} = \frac{-2}{\ln x + c} + 1  \\
                                & \Rightarrow & y = \frac{-2x}{\ln x + c} + x
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14310}%
$ 0=y(e)= \frac{-2e}{\ln e + c} +
e = \frac{-2e}{1 + c} + e = \frac{-2e+e+ce}{1+c} =
\frac{-e+ce}{1+c}= \frac{e(c-1)}{1+c}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14312}%
$ c=1$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14314}%

\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14321}%
$ \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y = \frac{y}{x} + \sqrt{1-\left( \frac{y}{x}\right)^2}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14327}%
$ y'=\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y = \frac{y}{x} + \sqrt{1-\left(
\frac{y}{x}\right)^2} = u + \sqrt{1-u^2}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14329}%
$ u+xu'=u + \sqrt{1-u^2}
\Rightarrow u'=\frac{\sqrt{1-u^2}}{x}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14331}%
$ u \neq \pm 1$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14337}%

\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14339}%
$ y=-x$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14343}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    u'= \frac{\sqrt{1-u^2}}{x}  & \Rightarrow & \frac{1}{\sqrt{1-u^2}}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}u = \frac{1}{x} \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \int \frac{1}{\sqrt{1-u^2}}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}u\;{\rm d}x =\int \frac{1}{x}\;{\rm d}x \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \int \frac{1}{\sqrt{1-u^2}}\;{\rm d}u =\int \frac{1}{x}\;{\rm d}x \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \arcsin u = \ln x + c \\
                                & \Rightarrow & u = \sin(\ln x + c)  \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \frac{y}{x} = \sin(\ln x + c)  \\
                                & \Rightarrow & y = x\sin(\ln x + c)
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14348}%
$ \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y + (2x-\frac{1}{x})y = x^2$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14350}%
$ p(x)=2x-\frac{1}{x}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_indisplay14352}%
$\displaystyle \mu(x)=e^{\int p(x)\;{\rm d}x} = e^{\int (2x-\frac{1}{x})\;{\rm d}x} = e^{x^2-\ln x} = \frac{e^{x^2}}{x}.$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14354}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
    \multicolumn{3}{l}{\frac{e^{x^2}}{x}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y + \frac{e^{x^2}}{x}(2x-\frac{1}{x})y = \frac{e^{x^2}}{x}x^2} \\
    \quad\quad\quad                     & \Rightarrow & \frac{e^{x^2}}{x}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y + \frac{e^{x^2}}{x}(2x-\frac{1}{x})y = xe^{x^2} \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}\left\lbrack \frac{e^{x^2}}{x}y \right\rbrack = xe^{x^2} \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \frac{e^{x^2}}{x}y = \int xe^{x^2}\; {\rm d}x \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \frac{e^{x^2}}{x}y = \frac{e^{x^2}}{2} + c \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & y = \frac{x}{2} + \frac{cx}{e^{x^2}}
    \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

$ x^2\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y + (1+x)y =
x^2\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}\left( \frac{A}{x} + B \right) + (1+x)\left( \frac{A}{x} + B \right)
= x^2\left( \frac{-A}{x^2} \right) + (1+x)\left( \frac{A}{x} + B \right)
= -A + \frac{A}{x} + B + A + Bx
= \frac{A}{x} + Bx + B.$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14391}%
$ y(x)=\frac{1}{x} + 1$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14393}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
            x^2\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y + (1+x)y = 0  & \Rightarrow & x^2\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y = -(1+x)y \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \frac{1}{y}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y = \frac{-1}{x^2} - \frac{1}{x} \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \int \frac{1}{y}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y\;{\rm d}x =\int \left(\frac{-1}{x^2} - \frac{1}{x} \right) \;{\rm d}x \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \int \frac{1}{y}\;{\rm d}y =\int \left(\frac{-1}{x^2} - \frac{1}{x} \right) \;{\rm d}x \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & \ln |y| = \frac{1}{x} - \ln x + c \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & |y| = e^{\frac{1}{x} - \ln x + c} \\
                                        & \Rightarrow & y = \frac{k}{x}e^{1/x}
            \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14404}%
$ x_{n+1}=2x_n + 2^{n-1}-\frac{n}{2}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14408}%
$ z_{n+1}=\frac{x_{n+1}}{2^n}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14410}%
$ z_{n+1}=\frac{x_{n+1}}{2^n} = \frac{2x_n + 2^{n-1}-\frac{n}{2}}{2^n}
= \frac{x_n}{2^{n-1}} + \frac{2^{n-1}-\frac{n}{2}}{2^n}
= z_n + \frac{1}{2} - \frac{n}{2^{n+1}}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_indisplay14412}%
$\displaystyle \left\lbrace
\begin{array}{rcl}
z_1-z_0 & = & \frac{1}{2} - \frac{0}{2^{0+1}} \\
z_2-z_1 & = & \frac{1}{2} - \frac{1}{2^{1+1}} \\
z_3-z_2 & = & \frac{1}{2} - \frac{2}{2^{2+1}} \\
& \vdots & \\
z_n-z_{n-1} & = & \frac{1}{2} - \frac{n-1}{2^{(n-1)+1}}
\end{array}
\right.$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14414}%
$ z_n-z_0=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}\left(\frac{1}{2} - \frac{i}{2^{i+1}}\right)
= \frac{n}{2} - \sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1} \frac{i}{2^{i+1}}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14416}%
$ z_n=z_0 + \frac{n}{2} - \sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1} \frac{i}{2^{i+1}}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_indisplay14418}%
$\displaystyle x_n = 2^{n-1}z_n = 2^{n-1}\left(z_0+\frac{n}{2} - \sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1} \frac{i}{2^{i+1}}\right).$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_indisplay14423}%
$\displaystyle \left\lbrace
\begin{array}{rcl}
A_1-A_0 & = & 2\left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)^{0} - 1 \\
A_2-A_1 & = & 2\left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)^{1} - 1 \\
A_3-A_2 & = & 2\left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)^{2} - 1 \\
& \vdots & \\
A_n-A_{n-1} & = & 2\left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)^{n-1} - 1
\end{array}
\right.$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14425}%
$ A_n-A_0=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}\left(2\left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)^{i} - 1\right)
= 2\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}\left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)^{i} - n
= 2 \frac{1-\left(\frac{N-1}{N} \right)^n}{1-\frac{N-1}{N}} - n
= 2 \left(1-\left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)^n\right)\frac{N}{N-(N-1)} - n
= 2N \left(1-\left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)^n\right) - n
$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14427}%
$ A_n=A_0 + 2N \left(1-\left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)^n\right) - n = 1 + 2N \left(1-\left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)^n\right) - n$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14430}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        \lim\limits_{N \to +\infty} \frac{A_N}{N} & = & \lim\limits_{N \to +\infty} \frac{1 + 2N \left(1-\left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)^N\right) - N}{N} \\
                & = & \lim\limits_{N \to +\infty} \frac{1}{N} + 2\left(1-\left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)^N\right) - 1 \\
                & = & 2 - 2\lim\limits_{N \to +\infty} \left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)^N - 1\\
                & = & 1 - 2\lim\limits_{N \to +\infty} \left(\frac{N-1}{N}\right)^N \\
                & = & 1-2e^{-1}
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_indisplay14435}%
$\displaystyle \left\lbrace
\begin{array}{rcl}
x_2-x_1 & = & 2^k \\
x_3-x_2 & = & 3^k \\
& \vdots & \\
x_n-x_{n-1} & = & (n-1)^k
\end{array}
\right.$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14437}%

\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14439}%
$ x_n=x_1 + \sum\limits_{i=2}^{n-1} i^k = 1 + \sum\limits_{i=2}^{n-1} i^k = \sum\limits_{i=1}^{n-1} i^k$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

$ k=1$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14447}%

\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14449}%

\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14455}%
$ S_n-S_{n-1} = n\cdot (n+1)$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14457}%

\lthtmlinlinemathZ

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_indisplay14459}%
$\displaystyle \left\lbrace
\begin{array}{rcl}
S_2-S_1 & = & 2\cdot 3 \\
S_3-S_2 & = & 3\cdot 4 \\
& \vdots & \\
S_n-S_{n-1} & = & n\cdot (n+1)
\end{array}
\right.$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14461}%

\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14463}%
$ S_n=S_1 + \sum\limits_{i=2}^{n} i(i+1) = 1\cdot 2 + \sum\limits_{i=2}^{n} i(i+1) = \sum\limits_{i=1}^{n} i(i+1)$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14465}%
$ \sum\limits_{i=1}^{n} i(i+1) = \sum\limits_{i=1}^{n} i^2+i = \frac{n(n+1)(2n+1)}{6} + \frac{n(n+1)}{2}
= \frac{(2n^3+3n^2+n)+(3n^2+3n)}{6} = \frac{2n^3+6n^2+4n}{6} = \frac{n(n^2+3n+2)}{3} = \frac{n(n+1)(n+2)}{3}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

\lthtmlindisplaymathZ

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14472}%
$ e^{at}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}y + e^{at}ay = e^{at}be^{-ct} \Rightarrow  \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}\left\lbrack e^{at}y \right\rbrack = be^{(a-c)t}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline14476}%
$ \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}\left\lbrack e^{at}y \right\rbrack = b \Rightarrow
e^{at}y = bt+k \Rightarrow y = bte^{-at} +ke^{-at}$%

            & = & \left( \frac{x_n}{x_{n+1}}-b_n \right)\left( \frac{x_{n-1}}{x_{n}}-b_{n-1} \right) \\
            &   & \quad\quad\quad +a_n\left( \frac{x_n}{x_{n+1}}-b_n \right)+b_n\left( \frac{x_{n-1}}{x_{n}}-b_{n-1} \right) \\\\
            & = & \left( \frac{x_{n-1}}{x_{n+1}}-b_n \frac{x_{n-1}}{x_{n}} -b_{n-1} \frac{x_{n}}{x_{n+1}} +b_nb_{n-1} \right) \\
            &   & \quad\quad\quad +\left(a_n\frac{x_n}{x_{n+1}}-a_nb_n\right) +\left(b_n\frac{x_{n-1}}{x_{n}}-b_nb_{n-1}\right)\\\\

        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%

\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14950}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{y}^{}}z=\frac{2}{y}z & \Rightarrow & \frac{1}{z}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{y}^{}}z=\frac{2}{y} \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \int \frac{1}{z}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{y}^{}}z \;{\rm d}y =\int \frac{2}{y} \;{\rm d}y \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \int \frac{1}{z}\;{\rm d}z =\int \frac{2}{y} \;{\rm d}y \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \ln |z| = 2\ln|y| + c \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \ln |z| = \ln y^2 + c \\
                                & \Rightarrow & z = k y^2
                                \textrm{ pour une constante }k
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath14952}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        ky^2=z=\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y       & \Rightarrow & \frac{1}{y^2}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y = k \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \int \frac{1}{y^2}\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y \;{\rm d}x =\int k \;{\rm d}x \\
                                & \Rightarrow & \int \frac{1}{y^2}\;{\rm d}y =\int k \;{\rm d}x \\
                                & \Rightarrow & -\frac{1}{y} = kx + k' \\
                                & \Rightarrow & y = \frac{-1}{kx+k'}.
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

        W(y_1,y_2)(x)   & = & \left\vert \begin{array}{cc} y_1 & y_2 \\y_1' & y_2' \end{array} \right\vert \\\\
                        & = & \left\vert \begin{array}{cc} \sin x^2 & \cos x^2 \\2x\cos x^2 & -2x\sin x^2 \end{array} \right\vert \\
                        & = & -2x \left( \sin x^2 \right)^2 -2x \left( \cos x^2 \right)^2  \\

\frac{e^{-\int p(x)\;{\rm d}x}}{J_0^2(x)}\; {\rm d}x$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath15045}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        y_2(x)  & = & J_0(x) \int \frac{e^{-\int p(x)\;{\rm d}x}}{J_0^2(x)}\; {\rm d}x \\
                & = & J_0(x) \int \frac{e^{-\int (1/x)\;{\rm d}x}}{J_0^2(x)}\; {\rm d}x \\
                & = & J_0(x) \int \frac{e^{-\ln x}}{J_0^2(x)}\; {\rm d}x \\
                & = & J_0(x) \int \frac{e^{\ln (1/x)}}{J_0^2(x)}\; {\rm d}x \\
                & = & J_0(x) \int \frac{1/x}{J_0^2(x)}\; {\rm d}x \\
                & = & J_0(x) \int \frac{1}{xJ_0^2(x)}\; {\rm d}x
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline15048}%
$ J_{1/2}'(x) = \frac{\cos x}{\sqrt{x}} - \frac{\sin x}{2x^{3/2}} = \frac{2x\cos x - \sin x}{2x^{3/2}}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath15050}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        J_{1/2}''(x) & = & \frac{2\cos x - 2x\sin x - \cos x}{2x^{3/2}} - \frac{3(2x\cos x - \sin
        x)}{4x^{5/2}} \\
                    & = & \frac{4x\cos x -4x^2\sin x - 2x\cos x - 6x\cos x + 3 \sin x}{4x^{5/2}}\\
                    & = & \frac{-4x\cos x -4x^2\sin x + 3 \sin x}{4x^{5/2}}
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath15052}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}{rcl}
        \multicolumn{3}{l}{x^2J_{1/2}''(x) + xJ_{1/2}'(x) + (x^2-\frac{1}{4})J_{1/2}(x)} \\\\
        \quad\quad  & = & x^2\frac{-4x\cos x -4x^2\sin x + 3 \sin x}{4x^{5/2}} + x\frac{2x\cos x - \sin x}{2x^{3/2}} + (x^2-\frac{1}{4})\frac{\sin x}{x^{1/2}} \\\\
                    & = & \frac{-4x\cos x -4x^2\sin x + 3 \sin x}{4x^{1/2}} + \frac{2x\cos x - \sin x}{2x^{1/2}} + (x^2-\frac{1}{4})\frac{\sin x}{x^{1/2}} \\\\
                    & = & -x^{1/2}\cos x -x^{3/2}\sin x + \frac{3 \sin x}{4x^{1/2}} \\\\
                    &   & \quad\quad\quad + x^{1/2}\cos x  - \frac{\sin x}{2x^{1/2}} + x^{3/2}\sin x -\frac{\sin x}{4x^{1/2}} \\\\
                    & = & \frac{3 \sin x}{4x^{1/2}} - \frac{\sin x}{2x^{1/2}} -\frac{\sin x}{4x^{1/2}} \\\\
                    & = & 0
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline15054}%
$ J_{1/2}(x)$%

\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}{rcl}
        y_2(x)  & = & J_{1/2}(x) \int \frac{1}{xJ_{1/2}^2(x)}\; {\rm d}x \\\\
                & = & \frac{\sin x}{\sqrt{x}} \int \frac{1}{x\left(\frac{\sin x}{\sqrt{x}} \right)^2}\; {\rm d}x \\\\
                & = & \frac{\sin x}{\sqrt{x}} \int \frac{1}{x\frac{\sin^2 x}{x}}\; {\rm d}x \\\\
                & = & \frac{\sin x}{\sqrt{x}} \int \frac{1}{\sin^2 x}\; {\rm d}x \\\\
                & = & \frac{\sin x}{\sqrt{x}} \cdot (-\cot x) \\\\
                & = & -\frac{\cos x}{\sqrt{x}}
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath15066}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        C_{n+1}-cC_n & = & \left\vert \begin{array}{ll} f(n+1) & g(n+1) \\f(n+2) & g(n+2) \end{array}\right\vert - c \left\vert \begin{array}{ll} f(n) & g(n) \\f(n+1) & g(n+1) \end{array}\right\vert \\\\
                     & = & \left\vert \begin{array}{ll} f(n+1) & g(n+1) \\-bf(n+1)-cf(n) & -bg(n+1)-cg(n) \end{array}\right\vert \\\\
                     &   & \quad\quad\quad - c \left\vert \begin{array}{ll} f(n) & g(n) \\f(n+1) & g(n+1) \end{array}\right\vert \\\\
                     & = & -b\left\vert \begin{array}{ll} f(n+1) & g(n+1) \\f(n+1) & g(n+1) \end{array}\right\vert - c \left\vert \begin{array}{ll} f(n+1) & g(n+1) \\f(n) & g(n) \end{array}\right\vert \\\\
                     &   & \quad\quad\quad - c \left\vert \begin{array}{ll} f(n) & g(n) \\f(n+1) & g(n+1) \end{array}\right\vert \\\\
                     & = & - c \left\vert \begin{array}{ll} f(n+1) & g(n+1) \\f(n) & g(n) \end{array}\right\vert - c \left\vert \begin{array}{ll} f(n) & g(n) \\f(n+1) & g(n+1) \end{array}\right\vert \\\\
                     & = & c \left\vert \begin{array}{ll} f(n) & g(n) \\f(n+1) & g(n+1) \end{array}\right\vert - c \left\vert \begin{array}{ll} f(n) & g(n) \\f(n+1) & g(n+1) \end{array}\right\vert \\\\
                     & = & 0
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

\cdot 0 = 0$%

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath15098}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        W(e^{\alpha t},e^{\beta t})(t)   & = & \left\vert \begin{array}{cc} e^{\alpha t} & e^{\beta t} \\\alpha e^{\alpha t} & \beta e^{\beta t} \end{array} \right\vert \\\\
                        & = & e^{\alpha t} \beta e^{\beta t}  - \alpha e^{\alpha t} e^{\beta t} \\
                        & = & (\beta- \alpha) e^{(\alpha + \beta)t}
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        W(t^{\alpha},t^{\beta})(t)   & = & \left\vert \begin{array}{cc} t^{\alpha} & t^{\beta} \\\alpha t^{\alpha -1} & \beta t^{\beta -1} \end{array} \right\vert \\\\
                        & = & t^{\alpha} \beta t^{\beta -1}  - \alpha t^{\alpha -1} t^{\beta} \\
                        & = & (\beta- \alpha) t^{\alpha + \beta - 1}
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ

y_2''(t_0) + p(t_0)y_2'(t_0) + q(t_0)y_2(t_0) = 0

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline15206}%
$ f, g$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline15208}%
$ h$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_indisplay15210}%
$\displaystyle \left\vert\begin{array}{ccc} f & g & h \\f' & g' & h' \\f'' & g'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert
=   f \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} g' & h' \\g'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert
- g \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & h' \\f'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert
+ h \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & g' \\f'' & g'' \end{array}\right\vert.
$%
\lthtmlindisplaymathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath15212}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}{rcl}
        \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}W(f,g,h)(t) & = & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}\left\lbrack
            f \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} g' & h' \\g'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert
          - g \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & h' \\f'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert
          + h \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & g' \\f'' & g'' \end{array}\right\vert \right\rbrack \\\\
          & = &   f' \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} g' & h' \\g'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert + f \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}\left\vert\begin{array}{cc} g' & h' \\g'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert \\
          &   & \quad\quad - g' \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & h' \\f'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert - g \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}\left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & h' \\f'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert \\
          &   & \quad\quad\quad\quad + h' \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & g' \\f'' & g'' \end{array}\right\vert + h \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}\left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & g' \\f'' & g'' \end{array}\right\vert \\\\
          & = &   f' \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} g' & h' \\g'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert + f \left( \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} g'' & h'' \\g'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert + \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} g' & h' \\g''' & h''' \end{array}\right\vert \right) \\
          &   & \quad\quad - g' \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & h' \\f'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert - g \left( \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f'' & h'' \\f'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert + \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & h' \\f''' & h''' \end{array}\right\vert \right) \\
          &   & \quad\quad\quad\quad + h' \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & g' \\f'' & g'' \end{array}\right\vert + h \left( \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f'' & g'' \\f'' & g'' \end{array}\right\vert + \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & g' \\f''' & g''' \end{array}\right\vert \right) \\\\
          & = &   f' \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} g' & h' \\g'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert + f \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} g' & h' \\g''' & h''' \end{array}\right\vert \\
          &   & \quad\quad - g' \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & h' \\f'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert - g \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & h' \\f''' & h''' \end{array}\right\vert \\
          &   & \quad\quad\quad\quad + h' \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & g' \\f'' & g'' \end{array}\right\vert + h \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & g' \\f''' & g''' \end{array}\right\vert \\\\
          & = &   \left( f' \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} g' & h' \\g'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert - g' \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & h' \\f'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert + h' \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & g' \\f'' & g'' \end{array}\right\vert \right) \\
          &   & + \left( f \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} g' & h' \\g''' & h''' \end{array}\right\vert - g \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & h' \\f''' & h''' \end{array}\right\vert + h \left\vert\begin{array}{cc} f' & g' \\f''' & g''' \end{array}\right\vert \right) \\\\
          & = & \left\vert\begin{array}{ccc} f' & g' & h' \\f' & g' & h' \\f'' & g'' & h'' \end{array}\right\vert + \left\vert\begin{array}{ccc} f & g & h \\f' & g' & h' \\f''' & g''' & h''' \end{array}\right\vert \\\\
          & = & \left\vert\begin{array}{ccc} f & g & h \\f' & g' & h' \\f''' & g''' & h''' \end{array}\right\vert
        \end{array}
        \end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath15214}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}{rcl}
        \multicolumn{3}{l}{\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{t}^{}}W(y_1,y_2,y_3)(t)} \\
        & = & \left\vert\begin{array}{ccc} y_1 & y_2 & y_3 \\y_1' & y_2' & y_3' \\y_1''' & y_2''' & y_3''' \end{array}\right\vert \\\\
        & = & \left\vert\begin{array}{ccc} y_1 & y_2 & y_3 \\y_1' & y_2' & y_3' \\-ay_1''-by_1'-cy_1 & -ay_2''-by_2'-cy_2 & -ay_3''-by_3'-cy_3 \end{array}\right\vert \\\\
        & = & -a\left\vert\begin{array}{ccc} y_1 & y_2 & y_3 \\y_1' & y_2' & y_3' \\y_1'' & y_2'' & y_3'' \end{array}\right\vert - b \left\vert\begin{array}{ccc} y_1 & y_2 & y_3 \\y_1' & y_2' & y_3' \\y_1' & y_2' & y_3' \end{array}\right\vert - c \left\vert\begin{array}{ccc} y_1 & y_2 & y_3 \\y_1' & y_2' & y_3' \\y_1 & y_2 & y_3 \end{array}\right\vert \\\\
        & = & -a\left\vert\begin{array}{ccc} y_1 & y_2 & y_3 \\y_1' & y_2' & y_3' \\y_1'' & y_2'' & y_3'' \end{array}\right\vert \\\\
        & = & -aW(y_1,y_2,y_3)(t)
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        (4xy^2-2x^3)\frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y=3x^2y-y^3    & \Rightarrow & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y=\frac{3x^2y-y^3}{4xy^2-2x^3} \\\\
                                    & \Rightarrow & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y= \frac{x^3 \cdot (3(y/x)-(y/x)^3)}{x^3 \cdot (4(y/x)^2-2)} \\\\
                                    & \Rightarrow & \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y=\frac{3(y/x)-(y/x)^3}{4(y/x)^2-2}
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline15744}%
$ u+xu' = y' = \frac{{\rm d^{}}}{{\rm d}{x}^{}}y = f(\frac{y}{x}) = f(u) =
\frac{3u-u^3}{4u^2-2}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline15746}%
$ u'=\frac{\frac{3u-u^3}{4u^2-2}-u}{x}
= \frac{\frac{3u-u^3}{4u^2-2}-\frac{4u^3-2u}{4u^2-2}}{x} =
\frac{\frac{-5u^3+5u}{4u^2-2}}{x}$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline15748}%
$ -5u^3+5u \neq 0$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline15760}%
$ u\equiv 1$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmlinlinemathA{tex2html_wrap_inline15766}%
$ y=xu=x$%
\lthtmlinlinemathZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}

{\newpage\clearpage
\lthtmldisplayA{displaymath15770}%
\begin{displaymath}\begin{array}[t]{rcl}
        u' = \frac{\frac{-5u^3+5u}{4u^2-2}}{x}  & \Rightarrow & \frac{4u^2-2}{-5u^3+5u}u' = \frac{1}{x} \\\\
                                                & \Rightarrow & \frac{4u^2-2}{u(1+u)(1-u)}u' = \frac{5}{x} \\\\
                                                & \Rightarrow & \left( \frac{2}{u} + \frac{1}{1+u} - \frac{1}{1-u} \right)u' = -\frac{5}{x} \\\\
                                                & \Rightarrow & 2\ln |u| + \ln|1+u| + \ln|1-u| = -5\ln |x| + c \\\\
                                                & \Rightarrow & \ln u^2 + \ln|1+u| + \ln|1-u| = \ln |x^{-5}| + c \\\\
                                                & \Rightarrow & \ln u^2\cdot|1+u|\cdot|1-u| = \ln |x^{-5}| + c \\\\
                                                & \Rightarrow & u^2\cdot|1+u|\cdot|1-u| = k|x^{-5}| \\\\
                                                & \Rightarrow & u^2\cdot|1+u|\cdot|1-u||x|^5 = k \\\\
                                                & \Rightarrow & u^2\cdot(1+u)\cdot(1-u)x^5 = k' \\\\
                                                & \Rightarrow & (y/x)^2\cdot(1+(y/x))\cdot(1-(y/x))x^5 = k' \\\\
                                                & \Rightarrow & \frac{y^2}{x^2}\cdot\frac{x+y}{x}\cdot\frac{x-y}{x}x^5 = k' \\\\
                                                & \Rightarrow & \frac{y^2(x^2-y^2)x^5}{x^4} = k' \\\\
                                                & \Rightarrow & xy^2(x^2-y^2) = k'
        \end{array}\end{displaymath}%
\lthtmldisplayZ
\lthtmlcheckvsize\clearpage}
